# Evidence ledger: sponsor_strict_2026

- Question: Estimate the number of distinct job postings published in the United States during calendar year 2026 (2026-01-01 to 2026-12-31) for new-graduate roles — summer internships plus full-time entry-level positions for new bachelor's or master's graduates with 0–1 years of experience, in professional (white-collar) occupations — whose posting text explicitly states that the employer will sponsor a work visa (for example "H-1B sponsorship available" or "visa sponsorship provided"). Count each posting once even if it appears on several job boards. Do not count postings that do not state sponsorship in their text, even if the employer has sponsored visas before or ticks a "will sponsor" box on a job platform, and do not count postings that rule sponsorship out.
- as_of: 2026-10-28
- Pinned target: Distinct unique job postings published in the United States during calendar year 2026 for new-graduate roles (summer internships plus full-time entry-level positions for new bachelor's or master's graduates with 0–1 years of experience) in professional white-collar occupations, whose posting text ex | unit distinct job posting | period 2026-01-01..2026-12-31 | publisher No official publisher; constructed estimate from job-board, employer, and administrative evidence

## Directions and pages

### d1 [related_series] Job-board aggregator (e.g., Indeed, LinkedIn, Glassdoor, Handshake, WayUp, Simplify, RippleMatch)

Hypothesis: Total unique new-graduate professional postings in 2026 multiplied by the share whose text explicitly mentions visa sponsorship yields the target. The share is the key bridge; aggregator totals give the base.

Queries issued by the searcher: new grad job postings 2025 total United States; share of job postings mentioning visa sponsorship; LinkedIn entry level jobs United States count 2025; CNBC Handshake 2.6% job postings visa sponsorship May 2026; Handshake State of the Graduate Class of 2025 total job postings number; Handshake 2026 entry-level internship postings 15% fewer number; share of job postings mentioning visa sponsorship United States percent; new grad job postings 2025 total United States number entry-level; Handshake "State of the Graduate" 2026 new grad job postings decline CNBC visa sponsorship 2.6%; LinkedIn entry-level jobs United States how many job postings 2026 count; share of job postings mentioning visa sponsorship United States; new grad job postings 2025 total United States number entry-level; Handshake CNBC 2.6% full-time US job postings visa sponsorship 2026; Handshake State of the Graduate class of 2026 job postings number entry-level data

### d2 [related_series] Sponsorship-focused job boards and trackers (e.g., MyVisaJobs, H1BGrader, Simplify, Built In, Levels.fyi job board)

### d3 [capacity_consumption] Large employers and campus recruiting teams

Hypothesis: Sum over major sponsoring employers of (new-grad openings × share of postings stating sponsorship) plus a long tail allowance. Employer recruiting volumes and sponsorship policies are observable; the share of their postings that state sponsorship is the bridge.

### d4 [base_rates] Labor-market survey / research

Hypothesis: The share of new-grad professional postings that explicitly mention sponsorship can be estimated from samples of postings (text-mining studies, Burning Glass/Lightcast-style analyses) and applied to the total new-grad posting base.

Queries issued by the searcher: percentage of job postings mention visa sponsorship study; Burning Glass H-1B job postings analysis text mining; Handshake CNBC visa sponsorship postings 2.6% 2026 international students entry-level; Indeed Hiring Lab share of job postings mentioning sponsorship methodology keyword; percentage of job postings that mention visa sponsorship study; Burning Glass H-1B job postings analysis share requiring sponsorship; CNBC Handshake visa sponsorship job postings 2.6% 2026 international students; Lightcast analysis job postings H-1B sponsorship mention percentage text mining; percentage of job postings mention visa sponsorship study; Burning Glass H-1B job postings analysis share requiring sponsorship; Handshake CNBC visa sponsorship postings 2.6% 2026 international students; F1 Hire International Talent Friendliness Index percentage job postings sponsorship-friendly 2025

### d5 [administrative] USCIS / Department of Labor

Hypothesis: H-1B registrations, LCA filings, and OPT/STEM-OPT approvals bound the population of new-grad roles that actually sponsor; the ratio of sponsorship-mentioning postings to actual sponsored hires (postings per sponsored hire) converts administrative flows into posting counts.

Queries issued by the searcher: H-1B cap registrations FY2027 USCIS number selected; LCA filings entry level wage level 1 2025 H-1B disclosure data; OPT approvals 2025 STEM OPT count USCIS; OFLC H-1B LCA certified FY2025 total number determinations annual report; USCIS FY2025 data OPT approved STEM OPT extension count report; job postings per hire ratio analysis Lightcast how many job postings per actual hire; H-1B registrations FY2027 number selected USCIS; LCA filings entry level wage level 1 2025 H-1B disclosure data; OPT approvals 2025 STEM OPT count USCIS; DOL OFLC FY2025 H-1B LCA filings total number annual report 620,000; "postings per hire" ratio job postings per hire research; USCIS OPT approvals fiscal 2025 count number employment authorization students; H-1B registrations FY2027 number USCIS; LCA filings entry level wage level 1 2025 OFLC disclosure data; OPT approvals fiscal year 2025 STEM OPT participants number USCIS report; share of job postings mentioning visa sponsorship percentage study

### d7 [shocks] Policy and macro analyst

Hypothesis: 2025–2026 immigration policy changes (H-1B fees, lottery rules, travel bans) and tech hiring cycles shift both the number of sponsoring employers and the propensity to state sponsorship in postings; quantifying exposure adjusts the base rate.

### b3 [administrative] d5

Hypothesis: The ratio of sponsorship-mentioning postings to actual sponsored hires (postings per sponsored hire) would convert the FY2025 114,806 initial H-1B approvals into a posting count, providing an independent route that does not depend on the disputed share.

- conflicting: ['p1', 'p15', 'p28'] Three independent measurements of 'share of US postings stating sponsorship' differ by 26x (2.6% vs 0.18% vs 0.1%). Differences are scope (Handshake is early-career/full-time only; JobShifu and Trylit
- corroborating: ['p79', 'p109', 'p264', 'p458', 'p937'] Indeed Hiring Lab: 0.14% of all US postings mention visa/green-card sponsorship, May 2025. Fourth independent all-posting measurement, consistent with JobShifu/Trylitos order of magnitude.

- corroborating: ['p852', 'p890', 'p902', 'p912', 'p711', 'p751', 'p838'] Handshake early-talent postings down ~15% in 2025 and ~2% in the July 2025-March 2026 window. Same platform, successive vintages; the 2026 window is the relevant one.

- corroborating: ['p860', 'p861', 'p862', 'p863', 'p864', 'p865', 'p866', 'p867', 'p868', 'p869', 'p870', 'p871'] Three rural NY counties: 14% of unique postings require 0-1 years experience, 67% list no experience. A local base rate for the 0-1 experience band, not a national total.
- corroborating: ['p899', 'p900', 'p872', 'p873'] Entry-level share of tech postings ~21-24% (2023-2025); NYC tech 0-3 years share 31-37%. Consistent order of magnitude for the entry-level share of professional postings.
- corroborating: ['p972', 'p973', 'p976', 'p977', 'p999', 'p1014', 'p1015', 'p1016'] Hires-per-posting and share of hires with no prior vacancy: 1.3 hires/vacancy (JOLTS), 42% of hires at zero-vacancy establishments, 16-22% of hires without a prior vacancy. Establishes that postings a
- corroborating: ['p792', 'p793', 'p213', 'p214', 'p374', 'p375'] 44.7% of entry-level postings stating a minimum ask for 2+ years; 8.5% of internships ask 3+. Relevant to how much of the 'entry-level' pool truly matches 0-1 years.
- corroborating: ['p795', 'p800', 'p819', 'p208', 'p377'] Relisting rates 3.6%-23% depending on window and definition. Relevant to deduplication: posting-event counts overstate distinct postings.
- corroborating: ['p124', 'p851', 'p126', 'p127', 'p128', 'p141', 'p142'] Sponsorship-filterable job databases: 836 company pages, 500k and 50k job databases, 100k career sites tracked. These are platform capacities, not counts of sponsorship-mentioning new-grad postings.

- m1 coverage: the whole target
- m2 coverage: the whole target

- m4 coverage: sum over channel of internship, full-time entry-level

Method details (computed by code):

- m2 formula_mc: p159 * a3; Monte Carlo P10 to P90 112,759 to 1,158,383; result unit (posting/year) * 1 year converted to posting; own 80% width x/÷3.21 (Monte Carlo of the formula inputs)
- Combination: 1 block(s), equal weight per block (methods whose inputs overlap by at least half form one block; backtest errors set widths, not weights): B1 = m2: 361,135 (width x/÷3.21, Monte Carlo of the formula inputs). Estimate = the only block.
- Interval: one block, so the interval is that block's own width (x/÷3.21).

Result: central **361,135** posting, 80% interval [112,672, 1,157,498] posting, basis monte_carlo (too few backtest years, so the interval width comes from Monte Carlo sampling of the formula inputs and reflects input uncertainty only), weight rule single_block, weight on methods that use assumptions 1.00

Unit of the estimate: posting (distinct job posting; total for 2026-01-01 to 2026-12-31)

## Published-value check (live use only)

Status: **no_candidates**. target period ends 2026-12-31, after as_of: it cannot have been published yet


## Final answer

The best computed estimate is 361,135 posting (80% model-based interval 112,672 to 1,157,498 posting) for distinct US job postings published in calendar year 2026 covering new-graduate roles (summer internships plus full-time entry-level positions for new bachelor's or master's graduates with [n]–[n] years of experience) in professional white-collar occupations whose posting text explicitly states visa sponsorship.

The only retained route (m2, weight 100%) multiplies the observed FY[n] initial-employment H-[n]B approvals, 114,806 distinct job posting, by an assumed 3 sponsorship-mentioning postings per approval (range 1 to 10). The approvals figure is observed; the postings-per-approval ratio is an analyst assumption, not measured. Other routes were discarded for missing inputs, so the estimate rests on a single block.

Main risk: FY[n] approvals are a lagged proxy, and the assumed ratio is unverified. Better evidence would be a direct count of deduplicated sponsorship-mentioning new-grad postings, or a measured postings-per-approval ratio.

Unit of the estimate: posting (distinct job posting; total for 2026-01-01 to 2026-12-31).

Key assumptions (proposed by the analyst model, not observed; code samples each one between the bounds shown):
- a3 Sponsorship-mentioning postings per initial-employment H-[n]B approval: 3 posting per approval, range 1 to 10; used by m2. Overturned by: Evidence that sponsorship-mentioning postings per approval are below [n] or above [n].

Method details (computed by code):
- m2 formula_mc: p159 * a3; Monte Carlo P10 to P90 112,759 to 1,158,383; result unit (posting/year) * 1 year converted to posting; own 80% width x/÷3.21 (Monte Carlo of the formula inputs)
- Combination: 1 block(s), equal weight per block (methods whose inputs overlap by at least half form one block; backtest errors set widths, not weights): B1 = m2: 361,135 (width x/÷3.21, Monte Carlo of the formula inputs). Estimate = the only block.
- Interval: one block, so the interval is that block's own width (x/÷3.21).

Input audit disputed: no method passed every check after the repair rounds, so this estimate uses methods that code could compute but the input audit questioned.
